scope. Original-contract full-coverage scores exist for E1 and E11.



\chapter{Campaign Lane Detail}\label{campaign-lane-detail}

This appendix holds the lane-by-lane record behind Chapter 8: provenance
of the training run, per-lane serving and scoping notes, the engineering
history behind individual figures, the per-lane narratives of the six
replacement scopes completed on 27 September 2026, the full E4 rerun
audit, and the per-lane caveats of the small-scale reruns. The
descriptive figures agree with Chapter 8, and the two reporting rules of
Chapter 8 (no pooling of original-contract and replacement-scope
results, no unsupported precision) apply throughout. The E11 uncertainty
calculation below is a new, reproducible analysis of surviving paired
verdicts. Six original files covering five previously missing basenames
are now preserved in the withheld source archive (Appendix B §B.11).
Receipt-level fields and the supplementary stored-payload checks are
distinguished below from historical claims whose raw trajectories,
training logs or supporting artifacts remain unvalidated; recovery and
count/hash checks are not new execution or regrading of the experiments.

\section{Actor identity, inference conditions and scoping
notes}\label{actor-identity-inference-conditions-and-scoping-notes}

The design consequence of evaluating one actor for seed variance is
specific: every suite number reported in Chapter 8 comes from one actor,
and where a lane sampled more than one completion per task it did so to
satisfy the suite's own protocol, not to estimate variance.

Inference conditions were reported per lane rather than imposed
globally, and the lanes differ widely. The recovered E10 native receipt
declares a temperature of 0, a 32,768-token context, 4,096 output tokens
per request, 3,600 seconds of wall time. It also declares
\longtok{max\_\allowbreak{}generation\_\allowbreak{}requests} = 1,967, a ceiling on generation
requests rather than a task count: the lane has 97 episodes. The earlier
report separately gives 414 native requests (405 in the main run and 9
in the continuation) and says the collector does not admit shorter
native conditions. Those execution counts and collector claims are not
fields of the recovered receipt, and the cited terminal-readiness source
remains unrecovered. The E11 run was sampled at temperature 0.2, top-p
0.95, 1,024 maximum output tokens and seed 1816, one sample per problem
across all 312 prompts. The point of recording them per lane is that the
sampling regime is part of the result, not a detail beneath it.

Two scoping notes complete the picture of what the actor has actually
been measured on. The E1 original-contract evaluation was a
one-checkpoint evaluation whose 731 generations were produced by a mixed
backend within a single run --- 476 Tinker generations and 255 Modal
vLLM generations --- and that backend mix is recorded in the original
receipt. Separately, two small probes exist at other parameter settings
but are not suite results and are not reported as such: a base-model
smoke run of the E11 harness measured zero scored tasks, and a four-task
pilot on the step-40 checkpoint (the last training step, saved
separately from \texttt{stepfinal}) returned four passes out of four
with zero extraction failures while carrying an unresolved outstanding
blocker --- the decontamination receipt identifier for that pilot does
not exist, and the split-manifest validator still fails on it.

\section{Training-run provenance}\label{training-run-provenance}

The earlier report attributed the adapter configuration to the local
Weights \& Biases directory for run \texttt{withheld-id}
(\longtok{withheld-training-log-directory}), the run receipt
\texttt{withheld\ actor-training\ receipt}, and Hugging Face checkpoint
branches. The original
\longtok{outputs/public\_\allowbreak{}portfolio\_\allowbreak{}2026-09-05/training\_\allowbreak{}identity\_\allowbreak{}verified.json}
is now recovered, with SHA-256
\longtok{e340d5fe41b7b028dd78ea9f01f6da787c7a8cb968f5a0ac1b103a92b546ee86}.
It records run \texttt{withheld-id}, dataset revision
\longtok{bd1f0db6056091e99d5e28e3a7b3d05d77b21876ad65e5b31bf6f1b470e73e5c},
the two training-suite IDs and hashes for \texttt{config.yaml},
\texttt{wandb-metadata.json} and \texttt{output.log}. It explicitly
labels this
\longtok{TRAINING\_\allowbreak{}CONFIG\_\allowbreak{}VERIFIED\_\allowbreak{}NOT\_\allowbreak{}CONTENT\_\allowbreak{}DECONTAMINATION} and
states that W\&B captured the dirty checkout's base commit but did not
upload the dirty source diff. The underlying W\&B files and local run
receipt have not been recovered in this package, so a new byte-level
match against those three recorded hashes is not established here. The
surviving E11 receipt verifies the evaluated base/adapter identity, but
does not reconstruct the missing training logs. Table 8.1 retains
detailed training fields as earlier reported except where the recovered
identity record directly corroborates them.

Four things remain unavailable or were never created, and they are
listed so that no reader fills them in:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{The exact training code.} The run records git commit
  \texttt{8fdade2f} with uncommitted changes that were never saved. The
  \texttt{pavlov\_portfolio} preset and its dataset builder do not exist
  at that commit; they first appear in commit \texttt{72b9336d3} on
  2026-08-14. The reward and data-pool code in Table 8.1 is therefore
  taken from the current tree, whose dataset hash does not match the
  recorded \texttt{bd1f0db6…}. It is the best reconstruction, not proof.
  The loss function and training loop are identical at
  \texttt{8fdade2f}.
\item
  \textbf{Which 80 training prompts were drawn.} The draw used
  \texttt{random.Random(809)} over the 512-row pool, but the pool
  construction that ran is the unrecoverable code above.
\item
  \textbf{The original sampler execution.} The public package does not
  include execution-specific operational access paths and cannot replay
  that original route.
\item
  \textbf{Any held-out training-time evaluation.} None was run
  (\texttt{evaluate\_heldout} false).
\end{itemize}

The run also departs from
\longtok{zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV\_\allowbreak{}EXPERIMENT\_\allowbreak{}PROTOCOL\_\allowbreak{}2026-08-09.md}.
The protocol plans a learning-rate search and a twelve-suite curriculum
with 1,200/128 prompt and response tokens. This run used two suites, 40
steps, 1,536/384 tokens and a learning rate of 1 × 10⁻⁵, and the
protocol does not mention it; only the budget matches.

\section{Serving conditions: format limits and the scope of the
stop-sequence
mechanism}\label{serving-conditions-format-limits-and-the-scope-of-the-stop-sequence-mechanism}

Table 8.2 reports substantial extraction and length failures; E11's
aggregate extraction count is verifiable in the surviving receipt and
E8's aggregate diagnostics are now corroborated by its recovered
original. The supplementary validation independently corroborates E8's
stored grade and finish-reason counts, but the 1,259 parse-none count
remains diagnostic-only. These checks do not validate every serving
field, response body or cited finish-era source. The reported absence of
role-token run-on in E1's single-turn Tinker generations does not
establish a necessary condition for the mechanism. The E4 bridge code
and recorded examples support a concrete failure mode, while the
corrected shim's nonzero outcome comes from one task against 100 bridge
trials. Task selection, serving path and caps differ, so the fraction of
the original aggregate zero caused by the defect remains unidentified.
For other bridge-served lanes exposure is possible, not demonstrated.
This matches the scope qualification in
\longtok{outputs/e1\_\allowbreak{}e14\_\allowbreak{}small\_\allowbreak{}scale\_\allowbreak{}2026-09-26/E4/BRIDGE\_\allowbreak{}STOP\_\allowbreak{}TOKEN\_\allowbreak{}FINDING.md}.

Three non-bridge failure attributions are corrected (Table E.1). E11's
150 extraction failures are attributed to the 1,024-token cap with
thinking on, not to answer format; E14's capped responses are correct at
34.5\% against 85.9\% for uncapped ones; and 593 of E1's 713 submitted
patches are corrupt for \texttt{git\ apply}. Lane detail is in §D.4 and
§D.5. The runners now record per-sample finish reasons and truncation
(E11, E14) and repair and record patch-apply status (E1), but no stored
score was regenerated.

\section{Strictly complete scopes: lane
detail}\label{strictly-complete-scopes-lane-detail}

The ledger lists E11 both among its ``replacement scopes'' and among its
original-contract results; Table 8.A lists it as original-public-subset.
It is one run and one result, not two.

\textbf{E8 --- LAB-Bench, public split.} The recovered
\longtok{outputs/public\_\allowbreak{}portfolio\_\allowbreak{}2026-09-05/labbench\_\allowbreak{}final\_\allowbreak{}diagnostics.json}
(SHA-256
\longtok{7d7d0bd47162534212f7526db548e1f5d42bbe16554bc2e29115f0d9cf5af941})
directly records the following per-category accuracy, correct counts and
coverage: TableQA 0.7869 (192/244); FigQA 0.5138 (93/181); ProtocolQA
0.2500 (27/108); SuppQA 0.1829 (15/82); LitQA2 0.1759 (35/199); DbQA
0.1308 (68/520); SeqQA 0.0333 (20/600); and CloningScenarios 0 (0/33).
No category is incomplete. Coverage per category is at or above 0.9878
everywhere, and the CloningScenarios coverage is exactly 1.0 with zero
correct. The receipt also records zero parser errors, 1,960 responses
marked ``sure'' against 7 marked ``unsure'', and a completion profile of
1,086 length-truncated finishes against 881 stop finishes. The
diagnostic's correct and incorrect fields partition all 1,967 rows, so
the headline is an end-to-end harness score over the full split. The
earlier account treats the 1,259 unparsed responses as incorrect and
supplies the conditional 450/708 sensitivity; that response-level
membership has not been independently regraded. The diagnostic binds a
native-receipt hash (\texttt{b08995d5…}) and a graded-responses hash
(\texttt{8a61def9…}). The supplementary Mac validation locates the
native receipt at the declared hash and checks 1,979 declared archive
members against both archive and local bytes. Independently tallying its
1,967 exported grade records reproduces 450 correct, 1,517 incorrect,
all category denominators and correct counts, 1,960 sure, 7 unsure,
1,086 length finishes, 881 stop finishes and zero parser errors. Those
exported records have no parsed-answer field, so they do not
independently establish the 1,259 parse-none count or its response-level
membership. No native scorer was rerun.

\textbf{E10 --- AgentDojo, benign utility scope.} The recovered native
receipt (SHA-256
\longtok{a40c90015200a389ce0767b14a78f9409330a0ae8615cb4822ef9b54656ebaa0})
records \longtok{missing\_\allowbreak{}evaluation\_\allowbreak{}ids} empty and
\longtok{native\_\allowbreak{}handled\_\allowbreak{}error\_\allowbreak{}count} zero. It carries run-manifest
hash
\longtok{00e4228f4b757dc32b69b74f05a88348151f13185b470c5f00e31fdaa830ef30}
and fixes the metric as \longtok{benign\_\allowbreak{}task\_\allowbreak{}utility}, benchmark
version v1.2.2, attack and defence null, \texttt{security\_evaluation}
false and \texttt{heldout\_claim} false. The earlier statement that all
three protocol fields were null is corrected (Table E.1). There is no
upstream benchmark commit field in the recovered receipt, so the earlier
attribution of commit \longtok{089ed468cf3ed0322acc66b0211f26d9d90dbf60}
to it is unsupported. The episode decomposition, banking 16, slack 21,
travel 20 and workspace 40, is absent from the native receipt but is
independently corroborated by the supplementary export's 97 selected
trace records. Their utility/error fields tally to 88 passes and zero
handled errors. All 97 evaluation IDs map uniquely to exact-hash
archived traces, selecting 94 from the original collection and 3 from
the explicit continuation; extra historical partial traces are not
pooled. The Mac validation also reports all 525 archive-member pins
matching. These are stored-trace checks, not re-execution of the native
evaluator or independent semantic grading. Its decontamination block
records \longtok{TRAINING\_\allowbreak{}INVENTORY\_\allowbreak{}ABSENT}; empty match lists without
the training inventory do not establish absence of overlap.

The following collector history is retained from the earlier report. The
supplementary validation locates the continuation terminal-evidence file
on the Mac and matches its declared hash, and the exported 94-plus-3
trace mapping supports the completed-episode accounting. It does not
reproduce the collector's 18 offline tests or export the full snapshot,
readiness and continuation-result contents, so the detailed repair and
test claims below remain historical. These sources are still absent from
the frozen repository. An earlier attempt reportedly terminated with 94
of 97 episodes and score null, missing workspace tasks 37 through 39.
The cause was a confirmed collector fault rather than a model failure:
verified task receipts recorded absolute \texttt{/cache/...} trace
paths, and the frozen summary recomputed those paths on the local host,
so every completed receipt failed comparison after extraction. The
repair was deliberately minimal --- the original archive and every
extracted receipt and trace byte are unchanged, and a narrowly scoped
adapter rewrites only the freshly computed
\longtok{task\_result.native\_\allowbreak{}trace\_\allowbreak{}path} to the verified canonical
remote path before the frozen summary compares it, leaving native task
code, environment, prompt, result parsing, utility values and
handled-error policy untouched. The revised collector passed 18 offline
tests, independently re-run in 0.507 seconds, and the continuation run
that closed episodes 37 through 39 was executed in the reported
continuation with the native wrapper hash
\longtok{11a8b61631390d9b20decd4263008e5a4c6f648a033d314384bac9e5d2456d77}.

\textbf{E11 --- VerilogEval.} The upstream NVlabs/verilog-eval harness
ran at revision \longtok{c498220d0a52248f8e3fdffe279075215bde2da6}, using
Icarus Verilog 12.0 at revision
\longtok{4fd5291632232fbe1ba49b2c26bb6b2bf1c6c9cf} and Verilator 5.050.
Pass verdicts were determined deterministically: for each pinned problem
the single \texttt{sample01} \texttt{sv-iv-test} log was read and marked
PASS only when it contained a full-line ``Mismatches: 0 in samples''
verdict, with missing or non-matching logs marked FAIL. The upstream
\texttt{sv-iv-analyze} summariser could not be used because it fails on
a missing optional langchain dependency; that failure is recorded in the
receipt and does not affect the aggregation, which reads the simulator
logs directly. An extraction failure writes an empty candidate file that
always fails.

\textbf{E11 design-cluster uncertainty.} The
\longtok{verifier.receipts.code-complete-iccad2023.log\_receipts} and
\longtok{verifier.receipts.spec-to-rtl.log\_receipts} mappings in the
surviving full receipt contain exactly the same 156 design IDs. Matching
by ID gives 54 designs passing both framings, 81 failing both, 13
passing code-completion only and 8 passing specification-to-RTL only.
The 312 prompt outcomes are therefore not 312 independent design
observations. The descriptive pass fraction is unchanged at (2 × 54 + 13
+ 8)/312 = 129/312. For uncertainty over design composition, resample
156 whole design pairs uniformly with replacement per replicate, retain
both observed outcomes, and calculate their summed passes divided by
312. With 200,000 replicates from Python
\texttt{random.Random(20261002)}, the 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles are
0.3429487 and 0.4871795, reported as {[}0.343, 0.487{]}. Quantiles use
linear interpolation at index (B − 1)p.~The standard-library script
\longtok{platform\_hybrid/experiments/scripts/e11\_\allowbreak{}design\_\allowbreak{}cluster\_\allowbreak{}bootstrap.py}
reproduces the calculation; the adjacent
\longtok{e11\_\allowbreak{}design\_\allowbreak{}cluster\_\allowbreak{}bootstrap.json} preserves method, seed,
receipt SHA-256, pair counts and all 156 paired verdicts, and the script
falls back to it when the ignored receipt is absent. Run it from the
repository root with
\texttt{python\ platform\_\allowbreak{}hybrid/\allowbreak{}experiments/\allowbreak{}scripts/\allowbreak{}e11\_\allowbreak{}\allowbreak{}design\_\allowbreak{}\allowbreak{}cluster\_\allowbreak{}\allowbreak{}bootstrap.py}.
The exact upper bound depends on drawing with \texttt{random.choices},
because the 97.5th percentile falls on the boundary between 151/312 and
152/312; drawing with \texttt{randrange} under the same seed gives
0.4839744. The script previously cited under
\longtok{outputs/PES\_\allowbreak{}Phase2\_\allowbreak{}Third\_\allowbreak{}Review\_\allowbreak{}2026-09-24/analysis/} was
never committed.

This interval assumes exchangeable underlying designs and conditions on
one checkpoint, one generation per framing and the observed protocol. It
does not estimate variation across training seeds, decoding seeds or
protocols, and does not establish generalization beyond comparable
designs. Conditional on the complete fixed benchmark and these recorded
outputs, 129/312 is simply the observed fraction. The parseable-only
129/162 remains descriptive: the receipt records 150 extraction failures
only in aggregate, not per-design extraction indicators, so it cannot
support a cluster-aware interval for that conditional rate.

\textbf{E11 failure attribution.} The 150 extraction failures are not
primarily answer-format failures (corrected; see Table E.1).
\longtok{run\_\allowbreak{}e11\_\allowbreak{}full} in
\longtok{zvf-program/flagship/modal\_\allowbreak{}e1\_\allowbreak{}e14.py} used
\texttt{max\_tokens} = 1,024 and did not pass \texttt{enable\_thinking},
so the model's template default, thinking on, applied. No per-sample
finish reason was recorded, but the receipt's 312,622 response tokens
over 312 samples give a mean of 1,002 of 1,024, so nearly every
generation reached the cap. On the 50 items of the 26 September
small-scale rerun, the receipt's verdicts give 23/50 and the same
trained adapter, served by vLLM with thinking off, temperature 0 and a
4,096-token cap, scores 33/50 with no extraction failures and every
response ending on a stop; the discordant pairs are 11 against 1 (exact
McNemar p ≈ 0.006). Engine and temperature changed along with the
budget, and 50 of 312 prompts is a subset, so 129/312 stays the
canonical score and no budget-corrected score is reported. The extractor
also stripped reasoning only when a closing
\longtok{\textless{}/think\textgreater{}} was present; for a response
truncated inside its reasoning it graded any
\texttt{module\ …\ endmodule} found in the draft, and only response
hashes were kept, so it cannot be determined how many of the 162
extracted candidates or the 129 passes came from reasoning drafts. The
runner now refuses extraction from an unterminated reasoning block,
makes the thinking mode explicit and records response text, finish
reason and truncation per sample; the stored result was produced before
those changes. The earlier unclustered Wilson intervals on pooled and
parseable-only outcomes are not used.

The E11 lane also carries the campaign's clearest example of a scoring
decision taken for evidential rather than numerical reasons. The
all-problems reference sweep --- running the shipped reference
implementation of every problem through both simulators --- returned 311
of 312. One problem,
\longtok{verilog\_eval/spec-to-rtl/Prob099\_\allowbreak{}m2014\_\allowbreak{}q6c}, fails to
elaborate against its own test bench under both simulators, with Icarus
reporting that ports \texttt{Y2} and \texttt{Y4} are not ports of
\texttt{good1}; no candidate implementation can pass that task, and the
upstream defect biases pass@k downwards by at most 0.64 percentage
points on the specification-to-RTL split (1/156) and by 0.13 percentage
points overall (129/311 − 129/312). Excluding it yields 129/311 =
0.4148. That corrected figure is \emph{not} canonical. The recorded
amendment of 2026-08-22 states the reason plainly: no immutable
benchmark-level justification was captured for the exclusion, so the raw
129/312 result is canonical and the 129/311 value is retained only as a
noncanonical sensitivity; no samples and no verifier artifacts were
changed by the amendment. The ledger's integrity check confirms that the
component split sums correctly to the canonical total.

\textbf{E14 --- Omni-MATH replacement scope.} The surviving consolidated
ledger and
\longtok{outputs/verification/LEDGER\_\allowbreak{}CODE\_\allowbreak{}CHECK\_\allowbreak{}2026-09-19.json}
record 4,426/4,428 accepted rows and accuracy 0.5131043831902395,
consistent with 2,271 passes among 4,426 judged rows. Counting the
remaining two as failures gives 2,271/4,428 = 51.29 per cent. The
recovered terminal note (SHA-256
\longtok{14b8308df159f9c86a3a58d724b8152a5f44f098c57a824637fcbe5ae6f3602a})
identifies \texttt{omni-01195-a6cbdc4ee3c8694b} and
\texttt{omni-02044-612ddacaa39d91fe} as the two unaccepted rows and
records that Omni-Judge outputs were truncated before an Equivalence
Judgement section. It states that both had recorded skip reasons, that
omission followed the native scorer, that no re-judge was performed and
that the lane closed with all 4,428 dispositions. This now supports the
terminal decision and explains the earlier parser-failure terminology at
receipt level. The supplementary Mac-side validation now inspects the
stored rows and exports the two exclusion records, each lacking the
Equivalence Judgement heading and carrying the recorded omission reason;
no mathematical answer was independently rejudged. The note's
\texttt{score\_impact} nevertheless writes
\texttt{2271/4428\ =\ 0.5131043831902395}; the decimal is instead
2,271/4,426. The recovered finish ledger repeats the inconsistent
pairing of 2,271/4,428 with 51.31\%. Both sources remain unchanged, and
the thesis preserves the corrected distinction between 51.29\% over all
dispositions and 51.31\% over judged rows (Table E.1). The supplementary
export identifies the disposition and judged-row payloads and the
scoring-recheck file on the Mac, with hashes. Its traversal records
4,428 valid rows in each of the disposition, judged-row and
new-judge-response JSONL files, with 4,426 accepted, 2,271 correct and
two skipped dispositions. The recorded native-score fields explicitly
distinguish denominator 4,426 from the all-4,428 diagnostic. The full
E14 rows are not exported here; the cloud check can independently verify
the exported arithmetic and two exclusion records, but cannot
independently recount all 4,428 rows from this export.

\textbf{E14 generation budget.} The actor cap was 2,048 tokens
(\longtok{zvf-program/flagship/public\_\allowbreak{}omni\_\allowbreak{}math\_\allowbreak{}native.py}), and 2,982
of 4,428 responses reached it. Recounting
\texttt{native\_dispositions.jsonl}, capped responses are correct in
1,029 of 2,982 (34.5\%) and uncapped ones in 1,242 of 1,446 (85.9\%)
(corrected reading; see Table E.1). Difficulty confounds the split,
because harder problems both run longer and fail more often, so it does
not estimate what a larger budget would score. It does mean the 51.29\%
headline is substantially a property of the budget and not a budget-free
capability estimate. The judge's 300-token cap is what truncated the two
unjudged reports. The runner did not record capped status; it now writes
a per-task \texttt{cap\_status.json} and an
\longtok{accuracy\_\allowbreak{}by\_\allowbreak{}actor\_\allowbreak{}cap} receipt field, without altering the
stored grades.

\section{Original-contract results: lane
detail}\label{original-contract-results-lane-detail}

\textbf{E1 --- SWE-bench Pro.} The denominator is the complete 731-row
test split of \texttt{ScaleAI/SWE-bench\_Pro} at revision
\longtok{7ab5114912baf22bb098818e604c02fe7ad2c11f}. Within that
denominator the run produced 713 native evaluations, fourteen generation
failures and four lost generation artifacts; those eighteen non-native
outcomes are retained in the denominator rather than trimmed, so the
figure is a rate over all 731 tasks and not over the subset that graded
cleanly. Those 713 native evaluations are mostly not evaluations of an
applied fix (corrected reading; see Table E.1). The prompt asked for
unified diffs with concrete line ranges but showed source without line
numbers and with elided regions, and the runner's diff validator checked
syntax but not that hunk-header line counts match the hunk bodies.
Parsing the stored \texttt{candidates.json} with
\texttt{git\ apply\ -\/-stat} rejects 593 of 713 patches as corrupt
(``corrupt patch at line \ldots{}''), and 133 carry a placeholder
\texttt{index\ 1234567..abcdefg} line. The official entry script runs a
plain \texttt{git\ apply\ -v} without \texttt{-\/-recount} and without
stopping on failure, so for those 593 instances the tests ran against
the unpatched base. With \texttt{-\/-recount}, 711 of 713 parse. Both
resolved instances are among the 120 that parse as submitted. Apply
status was not captured by the run, so whether every parseable patch
applied to its repository is not known. The 2/731 is retained as the
measured score, but it mostly reflects diff-header arithmetic rather
than issue resolution. The runner now recounts hunk headers before
submission, rejects count mismatches and records parse status for the
raw and submitted patch; the stored run predates that repair. The
declared licence for the dataset is null in the lane's own receipt. The
evaluation harness is \texttt{scaleapi/SWE-bench\_Pro-os} at commit
\longtok{ca10a60a5fcae51e6948ffe1485d4153d421e6c5}, and the 731-task
scoring run used the 731 official run scripts unmodified on native
\texttt{linux/amd64} Modal workers. A 1,200-second timeout, against the
official 120, was used only in an earlier single-task harness check that
ran the gold patch under arm64 QEMU emulation. That check is recorded as
not a model score, so the timeout deviation does not apply to the 2/731
figure.

\textbf{E2 --- FrontierSWE.} The scientific result remains partial at
1/17 tasks. The public package does not supply the original execution
route or privately held operational records.

\textbf{E4 --- BankerToolBench.} The surviving historical summaries and
recovered finish ledger report restored public artifacts and 1,175
manifest checks passing. The recovered ledger specifically reports 0/6
pass3/pass16 archive artifacts available, with only
\texttt{TINKER\_BRIDGE.json} verifying. This supports the historical
archive-loss account, not a fresh verification of the archive or a
replacement of the receipt-only 0.3115 metric.

\textbf{E7 --- BinaryAudit.} The recovered finish ledger preserves both
the terminal table's ``1/46 attempted'' and its superseded historical
snapshot's ``1/28 errored''. The terminal figure is retained in Chapter
8. Recovery makes both source statements directly inspectable, but the
ledger does not explain their denominator difference and the raw attempt
inventory has not been revalidated here.

\textbf{E9 --- MLE-bench.} Coverage without complete grading is not a
score, and the lane records it as such. Behind the coverage number sit
97 legacy receipts across 50 competition identifiers, of which 41 graded
receipts cover 40 unique competitions; the ledger's integrity check
verifies precisely that decomposition. The separate merged-vLLM arm
holds seven receipts, of which exactly one is a valid grade (H\&M,
0.02132) and six are invalid submissions, and the integrity check
confirms that arm is recorded separately with one graded row. One old
Spooky Author submission is absent locally although the later run's
complete submission is retained.

\section{Replacement-scope lane narratives, 27 September
2026}\label{replacement-scope-lane-narratives-27-september-2026}

Three things changed in how the lanes were executed, and each is
recorded as a deviation in the lane's own result file. First, where a
fail-closed controller blocked only on paperwork that the directive had
superseded, the lane used a thin runner that calls the benchmark's
native loop and native grader directly. Second, compute moved off AWS:
E2, E6 and E9 ran on Google Cloud virtual machines, E1's grading ran in
Modal sandboxes, and E13 ran locally. Third, every lane was served the
same actor, \texttt{campaign-actor-bf16} (the base model with the
seed809 adapter merged, §8.1), through vLLM with the non-thinking chat
template. No lane went through the Tinker bridge implicated in the E4
failure mechanism (§8.1.2, §8.5), so that defect is not expected here,
although the lanes' result files do not record their stop settings. The
shared endpoint served at a 32,768-token context. E5 used a dedicated
65,536-token copy of the same weights because its banking prompts exceed
32k.

\textbf{E1 --- SWE-bench Multilingual.} This run covers the 16 wave-10
recovery tasks and the 174 tasks that the 12 September waves never
reached, 190 tasks in all. The actor resolved one
(\texttt{phpoffice\_\_phpspreadsheet-3463}), which is 1/190 = 0.53 per
cent. Only 57 of the 190 produced a patch that applied and could be
graded: 120 failed at patch application or evaluation and 13 produced an
empty patch. Over graded tasks alone the rate is 1/57 = 1.75 per cent
({[}0.3, 9.3{]}). Thirteen tasks produced no generation and were scored
as failures: eight exceeded the 32,768-token serving context and five
hit provider errors. The earlier waves covered the other 110 tasks, with
4 resolved (3.64 per cent, {[}1.42, 8.98{]}). They used the same task
universe, adapter and grader family, but served at a 65,536-token
context, and their source-context collector was lost and had to be
re-implemented for this run. Because the contract differs, the two runs
are not combined, and the lane does not report a 300-task figure.

\textbf{E2 --- CORE-Bench, hard difficulty.} The actor answered every
question correctly on 27 of the 45 \texttt{codeocean\_hard} capsules, a
task accuracy of 0.600. At the question level it answered 53 of 79
correctly: 28 of 33 written questions and 25 of 46 vision questions.
Thirty-two tasks ended with the agent calling \texttt{finish}, ten at
the 150-turn cap and three at the 8,100-second time limit. The agent was
a thin tool loop (\texttt{bash}, \texttt{query\_image}, \texttt{finish})
rather than the benchmark's reference CORE-Agent scaffold, and the same
actor answered the vision queries. Thirty tasks never reached a machine
because of a local DNS outage, and a driver crash then interrupted ten
of their reruns. All thirty were rerun from scratch, and every aborted
attempt is archived. Because a leak of the answers would inflate this
figure, the lane checked for one. No agent command referenced any host
that serves the answers. Each capsule's \texttt{results/} directory is
stripped before the agent starts, and the answer keys are held off the
agent's machine. Two qualifications remain. Twelve of the 27 correct
tasks left no files in the capsule's results directory, which the native
grader does not require. Some vision questions are categorical and could
be guessed. With n = 45 the interval spans 27 percentage points.

\textbf{E5 --- Tau3.} Eight of 97 tasks succeeded, a pass\^{}1 of 0.082.
Twenty-five tasks ended in infrastructure errors: fourteen exceeded the
65,536-token context and eleven returned an empty assistant message.
Their cause is not established, and they count as failures. An
accidental resume of the run overwrote 24 of those error records in the
results file; their identifiers and causes were rebuilt from the run
log. Over the 72 tasks without such an error the native pass\^{}1 is
8/72 = 0.111, reported as a sensitivity figure only. The 20 tasks
cleaned before 19 September were rerun under the same protocol rather
than carried over, and scored 1/20. The user simulator was
\texttt{gpt-4.1-2025-04-14} at temperature 0.

\textbf{E6 --- WebArena.} All 812 tasks were run, and 90 succeeded,
giving 0.111. The figure is a lower bound. Of the 114 tasks whose native
evaluator calls an LLM judge, 108 could not be graded, because the judge
was unavailable for the run; the other six were resolved to zero by a
non-judge check. Forty-one tasks failed with a harness-level browser
timeout. Both groups count as failures. The graded-only rate is 90/704 =
0.128 ({[}0.105, 0.155{]}). If every ungraded task had passed, the rate
would be 198/812 = 0.244. With the 41 harness errors counted as
failures, the true figure lies between 0.111 and 0.244, and nearer the
lower end if the ungraded tasks succeed at the graded rate. The native
judge model (\texttt{gpt-4-1106-preview}) is retired, so a later regrade
would substitute a different judge. Most failures are early stops by the
native loop: the same action three times (237), the same typing three
times (103), the step limit (101), and three consecutive parse failures
(98). The agent was limited to 384 output tokens per step, the few-shot
examples were passed as user and assistant turns because the chat
template rejects mid-conversation system messages, and sites were not
reset between tasks, as in the native runner. The last 103
shopping-admin tasks were split across three containers of the same
official site image to finish in time.

\textbf{E9 --- ML-Dev-Bench.} Ten of the 34 released tasks passed their
native validators, 0.294. The agent was the benchmark's OpenHands
CodeActAgent, limited to 50 iterations. No task errored or timed out,
but only after one harness repair: OpenHands 0.21.0 hangs when it
truncates its context window, and a one-line upstream fix was backported
without touching prompts, validators or tasks. Thirty-nine of 1,045
model calls exceeded the 32,768-token context and were absorbed by that
truncation path. A relay between the agent and the actor forced the
non-thinking template and restored tool-call text that the server's
parser had dropped. An earlier four-task batch that hung before the
repair is excluded.

\textbf{E13 --- BALROG.} All 255 native episodes were played and graded,
and the BALROG score is 26.1 per cent progression (standard error 1.77;
a stratified bootstrap gives {[}22.7, 29.5{]}). BALROG's score is the
mean of six per-environment means, not a mean over episodes. By
environment the actor reached BabyAI 82.0 per cent (n = 50), Baba Is AI
25.0 (n = 120), Crafter 22.7 (n = 10), TextWorld 16.9 (n = 30), MiniHack
10.0 (n = 40) and NetHack 0.0 (n = 5). Sampling followed the recorded
protocol at temperature 1.0, but with thinking off where the original
run had it on. A 2,000-step cap was placed on NetHack for cost; all five
NetHack episodes ended on their own before it, so the cap never took
effect. Episodes stopped by an operational stop or a hung worker were
restarted from scratch with fresh seeds. The result file records 262
episode attempt records for the 255 scored episodes, of which 7 are
marked killed for restart (\longtok{n\_killed\_\allowbreak{}for\_\allowbreak{}restart\_\allowbreak{}records});
its caveat text instead counts 11 restarts (one NetHack and five Baba Is
AI episodes at the 01:40 UTC stop, plus five hung workers), and the file
does not reconcile the two counts. If hangs correlate with episode
difficulty, that is a mild selection risk. The run used macOS arm64
builds of the pinned sources rather than the Linux image. The 26.1
figure is on a different scope from the 26-episode small-scale E13 rows
of Tables 8.A and 8.B and is not compared with them.

The launch-pending and quota labels of 19 September no longer describe
any lane.

\section{E4 rerun: reported audit and causal
limits}\label{e4-rerun-reported-audit-and-causal-limits}

The recovered finish ledger records E4 as \texttt{CLOSED\_PARTIAL},
preserving the 19 September close-out. It reports the pass3/pass16
archive as missing (0/6 archive artifacts; only
\texttt{TINKER\_BRIDGE.json} verifies), and keeps the 0.3115 recovery
metric as receipt-only evidence. Its updates through 21 September state
that the full 100-trial rerun had not launched. That snapshot cannot
substantiate the later completed rerun. The surviving 26 September E4
result's \longtok{prior\_\allowbreak{}base\_\allowbreak{}run} field instead reports a 21 September
run with 100 trials, one error and mean reward 0.0. Its source job
receipt remains unrecovered in this package, so the detailed account
below remains historical rather than a fresh trajectory-level
verification.

The earlier report describes the rerun as the base model with a
zero-initialised LoRA snapshot, equivalent to base sampling with no
training steps, through the Modal bridge \texttt{/v1/responses} path at
\texttt{-\/-n-concurrent\ 2} It reports 100 of 100 trials, one
\texttt{NonZeroAgentExitCodeError}, a mean reward of exactly 0.0, all
one hundred trials in the 0.0 bucket and a runtime of 3 hours 29
minutes. The surviving
\longtok{outputs/e1\_\allowbreak{}e14\_\allowbreak{}small\_\allowbreak{}scale\_\allowbreak{}2026-09-26/E4/result.json}
(\longtok{prior\_\allowbreak{}base\_\allowbreak{}run}) corroborates the 100 trials, one error, zero
mean and reward buckets, and identifies the serving path. The exact
launch configuration, named error type, cap and runtime are not
established by that secondary field and cannot be rechecked against the
unrecovered
\longtok{outputs/e4\_\allowbreak{}banker\_\allowbreak{}toolbench/official\_\allowbreak{}repo\_\allowbreak{}ff6db552/jobs/btb-banking-tasks-tinker-bridge/result.json}.
None of those later-run details is attributed to the recovered
pending-state snapshot.

The historical audit reports a failure mechanism across the one hundred
trajectories. Agents reportedly terminate after 2 to 14 steps, with a
median of about 6, and produce no \texttt{deliverables/} directory; the
verifier consequently short-circuits to 0.0 on the message ``No
deliverables found''. Twenty-seven or more of the trajectories end in
role-token degeneration of the form \texttt{user\ user\ assistant\ ...}
repetition. The record at the time read this as the base model being
unable to sustain the opencode tool-use loop. A later bridge check
exposes an alternative explanation, so that reading is not identified by
this uncontrolled run. The bridge's \texttt{/v1/responses} path passed
no stop sequences and decoded with special tokens removed, so generation
ran past the end-of-turn token into invented \texttt{user},
\texttt{tool\_response} and \texttt{assistant} turns, which is exactly
the role-token degeneration observed. The corrected shim scored 0.4087
on \texttt{btb-19b3361c}, but that single-task success is compared with
100 bridge trials under different tasks, serving path and caps. The
contrast supports a plausible mechanism that can cause zero-reward
trajectories; it does not quantify the defect's contribution to the full
zero or rule out model limitations. The reported no-deliverables check
is consistent with fail-closed grading, but the absent raw trajectories
prevent a fresh verification of the cause of every zero. No unconfounded
full-suite BankerToolBench capability estimate exists for this actor
family; the other measurement is the six-task subset of Tables 8.A and
8.B. The same bridge served E2, E5, E7 and the E9 streaming arm, so
Table 8.2 marks those lanes as exposed to the same defect. In
particular, the rerun's 0.0 must not be set against the original
single-task outcome as though the two were a controlled pairing: they
are different configurations under different runtime conditions, and the
campaign makes no comparison between them.

The historical rerun report records 16.35 million prompt tokens and 2.18
million completion tokens. These token details cannot be rechecked
against the absent job receipt. The surviving
\longtok{outputs/e1\_\allowbreak{}e14\_\allowbreak{}small\_\allowbreak{}scale\_\allowbreak{}2026-09-26/E4/result.json}
(\longtok{prior\_\allowbreak{}base\_\allowbreak{}run}) repeats the 100 trials, one error and zero
mean; its \longtok{verifiable\_\allowbreak{}from\_\allowbreak{}raw} flag is a historical assertion
and does not establish raw-artifact availability in this repository.

\section{Externally blocked lanes and verification
limits}\label{externally-blocked-lanes-and-verification-limits}

Seven original contracts remain externally blocked: SDAB, BinaryAudit,
LifeSciBench, AgentHarm, AppBench, OpenReward and FrontierMath. These
are coverage limitations, not scored failures or evidence about model
ability. Replacement scopes retain their separate names and
denominators. Operational access correspondence, contact destinations,
account capacity, request identifiers and account costs are omitted from
this public derivative.

The historical record reports eleven deterministic checks covering
selected arithmetic, coverage and source bindings. This is not an
independent re-execution of the benchmarks, and it does not establish
universal receipt availability. The original campaign recovery and
stored-payload checks have the bounded scope stated in Appendix B.

\section{Small-scale reruns: detail and per-lane
caveats}\label{small-scale-reruns-detail-and-per-lane-caveats}

Each lane's items, raw outputs and grader logs are kept so that every
figure in Tables 8.A and 8.B can be recomputed.

For E12 the 151 rubric items come from only six generated applications,
so they are not independent. The cluster-level test is an exact
sign-flip test over the six per-app pass-rate differences (−0.042,
+0.030, 0.000, −0.160, 0.000, −0.042), and it gives p = 0.25. The E12
grader is an LLM judge that is the same \texttt{Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B}
base model as the base arm, so self-preference could favour the base
arm. Two judge versions disagree widely (0.536 against 0.808 on the
Tinker base run; \texttt{E12/result.json}). The E12 rates and difference
are therefore judge-dependent, and no independent judge was run. For E4,
all six tasks score 0 in both arms except \texttt{btb-19b3361c}; a
Wilson interval is not valid for a mean of fractional {[}0, 1{]}
rewards, and a bootstrap over one nonzero difference in six is
degenerate. For E13, in 22 of the 26 episodes the progression is
identical in the two arms. No lane-level difference is interpreted as
evidence that the adapter improves or degrades performance unless its
test excludes zero, and none is extrapolated to the full suite.

The principal caveat for each lane, as recorded in its result file, is
listed below; the full list is in each lane's \texttt{result.json}.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{E1.} New base-model arm; not comparable to or poolable with
  the original 2/731. Original inference temperature was 0.2; this
  public subset cannot replay its private execution route.
\item
  \textbf{E2.} Substitute benchmark; never pool with or present as the
  FrontierSWE lane score (original: 1/17 tasks, replay 0.8628). Original
  execution access is outside this public package.
\item
  \textbf{E3.} New base-model arm; never pool with or present as the
  original SDAB/seed809 lane score.
\item
  \textbf{E4.} Small n=6 of 100; one nonzero task, so no interval is
  reported (the result file's Wilson95 on summed fractional reward is
  not valid for a {[}0,1{]} mean).
\item
  \textbf{E5.} n=10, not the requested 20: the E4+E5 6M Tinker-token cap
  was exhausted (E4 1.56M, abandoned APEX attempt 2.33M, tau2 2.05M).
\item
  \textbf{E6.} Tasks drawn by random.Random(20260926).sample from all
  128 sorted miniwob env ids; episode seeds 0-2.
\item
  \textbf{E7.} New base-model arm; never pool with or present as the
  original lane score.
\item
  \textbf{E8.} New base-model arm; not comparable to or poolable with
  the seed809-adapter 1967/1967 run (22.88\%); the public subset cannot
  replay the original execution route.
\item
  \textbf{E9.} Competition pool: 6 low-split competitions with small
  non-image-heavy data (detecting-insults, jigsaw-toxic,
  leaf-classification, nomad2018, random-acts-of-pizza, spooky); 5 drawn
  by random.Random(20260926).sample. n=5 so the CI is very wide.
\item
  \textbf{E10.} New base-model arm on AgentHarm itself; the
  seed809-adapter lane figure (AgentDojo benign utility 88/97 = 90.72\%;
  original execution route not supplied publicly) is a different
  benchmark and not comparable.
\item
  \textbf{E11.} New base-model arm; not comparable to the retained
  adapter receipt 129/312 = 41.35\% (that run used thinking-default
  template, temperature 0.2, all 312).
\item
  \textbf{E12.} Public tasks, not held-out. Label mandatory. New
  base-model arm; never pool with the original lane.
\item
  \textbf{E13.} New base-model arm; not comparable to or pooled with the
  original seed809-LoRA E13 plan (21-episode BabyAI admission set).
\item
  \textbf{E14.} New base-model arm; the prior 2271/4428 = 51.29\% figure
  (51.31\% on the official scorer's 4426 judged rows) is not comparable
  (different actor, full split).
\end{itemize}



\chapter{Corrections to Internal Source
Documents}\label{corrections-to-internal-source-documents}

The numbers in this thesis are drawn from the project's internal source
documents: the unpublished P1--P8 reports and their section files, audit
summaries and result files. Some of those documents contain errors --- a
wrong sign, a test applied to the wrong unit of analysis, an interval
that does not describe its own estimate, or a label that does not match
the rows it covers. The chapters report only the corrected value and
mark it ``(corrected; see Table E.1)''. This appendix records, for each
correction, what the source states, what the thesis uses instead, and
the basis for the change, so that a reader who opens the source can
reconcile it with the thesis. Caveats about the evidence itself, as
opposed to errors in a source document, stay in the chapters. Paths are
relative to the repository root; ``sections/'' is
\longtok{platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/} and ``results/'' is
\longtok{platform\_hybrid/experiments/results/}.

\section{Evidence-scope repairs after the 2 October
review}\label{evidence-scope-repairs-after-the-2-october-review}

The following corrections were made without new model training or
regenerated historical receipts.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{ZVF definition (§§4.3, 6.3.1, 7.1, 7.3).} Exact ties and
  sample variance ≤ 10⁻⁶ are now separate metrics. On the 600 stored
  groups, seeded micro-jitter changes exact ties from 95 to zero, but
  leaves 95 below the declared threshold. The epsilon sweep and source
  hashes are recorded in
  \longtok{pcd\_\allowbreak{}vs\_\allowbreak{}zvf\_\allowbreak{}tolerance\_\allowbreak{}analysis.json}.
\item
  \textbf{Controller interpretation (§7.3).} Zero rescue after plugging
  \ensuremath{\hat{p}} = 0 or 1 into a Bernoulli model, or resampling eight identical
  rewards, is estimator-imposed. It does not show latent certainty or
  impossibility of recovery with independent continuations.
\item
  \textbf{Loss provenance (§§1.1, 4.2--4.3, 6.3.1).} Zero-KL claims are
  row-specific. TRL nonzero-KL configuration evidence, adaptive-KL
  defaults, managed unknowns and actor source-binding gaps remain
  visible; zero reward-relative advantage does not imply a zero full
  optimiser update.
\item
  \textbf{PPO and group-size scope (§6.5.5; Chapter 9).} The unsaturated
  rerun is present in the synthesis. Its five-seed PPO comparison is
  baseline-specific and test-sensitive (paired-t p = 0.016; exact p =
  0.0625), with unequal prompt exposure and near-cap lengths. G8 minus
  G2 remains inconclusive, without equivalence.
\item
  \textbf{E11 uncertainty (§8.2; Appendix D).} The point score stays
  129/312. Resampling the 156 paired underlying designs gives a 95\%
  percentile interval {[}34.3\%, 48.7\%{]}, replacing the iid-312 Wilson
  interval. The conditional extracted-only score remains descriptive.
\item
  \textbf{E4 mechanism (§8.5; Chapter 9).} Missing stops are a verified
  defect, but the one corrected-path task changes several conditions
  together and cannot attribute the full 100-trial zero to stops alone.
\item
  \textbf{Source access (Appendix B §B.11; recovery update, 3 October).}
  Six exact originals covering all five previously missing basenames
  were recovered from the Mac, including both pending-state snapshots,
  and preserved with hashes in a separate private package. All remain
  absent from the frozen checkout. Supplementary read-only checks
  corroborate stored E8/E10 grades and E14 disposition/denominator
  reporting; they do not rerun scorers or establish content
  decontamination. Training raw logs and E4 raw rerun evidence remain
  unresolved. The E14 denominator and E10/E4 attribution repairs above
  change no scientific counts or existing thesis denominator arithmetic.
\item
  \textbf{Fraud side-study intervals (§F.4).} Sensor-minus-full AUC and
  accuracy intervals are {[}−0.0320, −0.0174{]} and {[}−0.0160,
  −0.0109{]}, reversing and reordering the source's opposite-direction
  intervals.
\end{itemize}



\chapter{Applied Side Study: Credit-Card Fraud Detection
(P8)}\label{applied-side-study-credit-card-fraud-detection-p8}

\textbf{Scope statement.} This appendix reports a side study. It uses
synthetic data and sits outside the RL contribution of this thesis. The
dataset is generated with scikit-learn's \texttt{make\_classification};
the language-model arm is trained by supervised fine-tuning, not by
reinforcement learning; and no result in this appendix is used to
support any GRPO, PPO, ZVF, group-size or campaign claim elsewhere in
the report. The study is retained, in condensed form, as the P8 slot of
the internal report series and as one worked example of measuring a
claimed capability rather than accepting its label.

The study compares a gradient-boosted tree with a fine-tuned language
model on credit-card fraud detection. Its conclusion is that the tree
keeps the real-time scoring seat, and that the language model's
plausible roles lie in tasks the tree cannot perform at all.

\section{Dataset, splits, and the two
arms}\label{dataset-splits-and-the-two-arms}

The dataset is synthetic: \texttt{make\_classification} (seed 42),
50,000 transactions, 20 anonymised numeric features V1--V20 (10
informative, 2 redundant), two clusters per class, class separation 0.8
and \texttt{flip\_y=0.01}, with a realised fraud rate of 1.44\%. Four
per-row aggregates (mean, standard deviation, maximum, minimum) are
appended, giving 24 features. The data reproduce class imbalance and
anonymised feature shape, and none of the temporal drift, verification
latency or adversarial adaptation of real card-fraud data.

Arm & Eval split & \emph{n} & Accuracy & ROC-AUC \\

XGBoost (scorer) & full test & 10,000 & n/a & 0.7955 \\
Qwen3.5-4B SFT & positive-enriched test & 500 & 0.7920 & 0.4827 \\
\end{longtable}

\emph{Source:
platform\_hybrid/experiments/results/quick\_20260704/qp8\_fraud.tsv.}

The two AUCs sit on different held-out splits, so this is not a
like-for-like ranking. The language model's AUC of 0.4827 is
chance-level ranking: its Hanley--McNeil 95\% CI, with 100 positives and
400 negatives, is {[}0.42, 0.55{]} and includes 0.5. Its accuracy of
0.792 is slightly \emph{below} the 0.800 that a constant ``legitimate''
answer would score at 20\% prevalence, so it is not evidence of
discrimination. The tree's AUC interval is {[}0.75, 0.84{]}, with 144
positives among 10,000 test rows. The tree's precision 0.723 (34/47,
Wilson 95\% CI {[}0.58, 0.83{]}), recall 0.236 (34/144, {[}0.17,
0.31{]}) and F1 0.356 at the default threshold come from the released
script's own run (AUC 0.7942, 0.49 s training, about 6 ms inference per
10,000 rows), which uses a slightly different configuration from the
0.7955 artefact. An older record of 21 June 2026 (XGBoost AUC 0.975,
language model 0.948) could not be reconstructed and is not used.

\section{Why the scoring seat stays with the
tree}\label{why-the-scoring-seat-stays-with-the-tree}

Four operational arguments hold even at AUC parity:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Latency.} Card authorisation needs a score within tens of
  milliseconds; the tree takes about 6 ms per 10,000 rows, against
  roughly 300 ms for a language-model pass.
\item
  \textbf{Cost.} The released cost table puts the synchronous
  language-model path at about 35 times the per-transaction cost of the
  tree path.
\item
  \textbf{Calibration.} Downstream threshold and expected-loss rules
  consume the score as a probability; verbalised language-model
  confidences are poorly calibrated.
\item
  \textbf{Prompt injection.} A model that reads attacker-controlled text
  fields in a synchronous decision loop opens an injection channel that
  a tree scoring numbers does not have.
\end{itemize}

\clearpage

\longtok{fraud\_data.csv} and score a subset of it; they are
within-protocol relative comparisons only. The leakage-free split gives
0.7955 & p8\_evidence.tex \\
Cost per fraud caught (\$0.50/alert, \$100 missed-fraud loss) & Raw
features \$2.68; sensor-augmented \$3.28; sensor-only \$14.92; the
sensor never pays for itself in the 25-cell (σ, L) grid &
p8\_evidence.tex \\
Sensor-noise break-even & \$5.29 (oracle), \$26.44 (σ ≤ 0.02), \$36.40
(σ = 0.05) & p8\_\allowbreak{}noisy\_\allowbreak{}sensor.tex \\
\end{longtable}

\endgroup

\section{Proposed roles for the language
model}\label{proposed-roles-for-the-language-model}

The study argues, without measuring, that the language model's useful
roles are tasks the tree cannot enter: document and image fraud through
a vision-language model, drafting compliance narratives (Suspicious
Activity Reports under 31 C.F.R. § 1020.320; adverse-action reasons
under 12 C.F.R. § 1002.9), cold-start triage of new fraud typologies
before labels exist, and post-score investigation of the alert queue.
The proposed architecture keeps XGBoost as the scorer and uses the
language model as a sensor (typed features, no authority) and a scribe
(narratives conditioned on the tree's own attributions, with a human
signature). The ``85 times cheaper than an analyst'' triage figure is an
internal estimate from June-2026 token prices and assumed investigation
times, and has not been validated.

\section{Limitations}\label{limitations}

The dataset is synthetic and single-generator, so no number here is a
production expectation, and there is no cross-institution or
public-benchmark replication. The challenger is one model family, one
serialisation and one fine-tuning budget. Of the proposed roles, only
the scorer comparison carries a measurement of the study's own; the
others rest on external literature and regulatory text, and nothing in
the compliance discussion is legal advice.



\chapter{C1 Evidence and Case Ledger}\label{c1-evidence-and-case-ledger}

Benchmark reference errors, ambiguous questions and equivalent units
limit what this diagnostic can establish. Its frozen parser-defined
result is \textbf{11/64 questions (17.19\%; Wilson 95\% interval
9.88-28.21\%)}. Assistant-assisted reconciliation corroborates
\textbf{three numeric recovery witnesses on the same 64-question
denominator}. This 3/64 count (4.69\%) describes the support found under
a conservative review rule; it does not replace the frozen endpoint or
estimate a population recovery rate. The other eight parser events
include three convention-dependent interpretations, four materially
ambiguous cases and one match to an incorrect reference answer.
{[}S1-S4{]}

The study contains no training update. It does not measure useful
reinforcement-learning gradients, reasoning improvement, controller
superiority, held-out accuracy or general model capability. The prompts
are public GSM8K training questions and are fresh only to this campaign.
Historical V2 evidence remains separate and unpooled.

The following ledger accounts for every frozen primary event. C labels
use the zero-based selected index. Fresh indices identify all
parser-success outputs among the 24 fresh draws. Complete question IDs,
reference hashes, response hashes and review notes accompany the
editable case ledger. {[}S3{]}

C00 Santana presents, 676 & 8 & 12, 15, 23 & Corroborated \\
C07 Suzanne bread, 912 & 26 & 2, 5, 10, 15, 16, 21 & Corroborated \\
C08 Citations, 923 & 4 & 0, 1, 3, 15, 23 & Convention / disagreement \\
C19 Pet owners, 1482 & 100 & 4, 16 & Ambiguous \\

C35 Checkpoints, 2593 & 6 & 6, 7, 10, 22 & Wrong reference \\
C37 Walking distance, 2660 & 36 & 0, 22 & Ambiguous \\

C47 Cake and ham, 3203 & 12 & 0, 4, 5, 9, 22 & Ambiguous \\
C50 Reggie marbles, 3255 & 1 & 2, 15, 22 & Ambiguous \\
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

\textbf{C00 Santana presents.} Gold 8 is (4 second-half birthdays+7
Christmas gifts)-3 March gifts. Both reviews support the quantity. Fresh
12 matches 8 through a false 8-0 argument; fresh 15 and 23 give the
correct 11-3 calculation.

\textbf{C07 Suzanne bread.} 3 slices per meal × 2 weekend meals × 52
weeks/12 slices per loaf = 26. Sharing one slice does not require
knowing the number of daughters. All 8 initial 90/91 claims use seven
days per week; every fresh 26 uses the correct weekend schedule.

\textbf{C08 Citations.} Other citations most naturally means the
combined non-parking count: L+L+2(L+L) = 24 gives 4. All initial 6
claims substitute twice one category. First treats that as material
ambiguity; second supports 4. Ordinary-reading support is stronger than
the per-category alternative, but disagreement remains and this is not
added to the corroborated recovery count.

\textbf{C19 Pet owners.} Half of pet owners plus disjoint exhaustive
dog/cat ownership gives 100; half of all citizens gives 300 under the
same partition. Overlap allows other values. The denominator ambiguity
is material even if ordinary classroom partition assumptions are
granted.

\textbf{C20 Raft capacity.} The ordinary exact additive-space model
makes 14 jackets occupy 7 person-spaces; 8 jackets reduce 21 by 4,
yielding 17. Arbitrary real packing/safety possibilities do not by
themselves constitute a defect. First conservatively withheld support;
second accepts the conventional model. Retain that disagreement and
conditional event support only in the ledger.

\textbf{C29 Fundraiser.} Next 4 days naturally names a single comparison
period, making 950 the four-day total and shortfall 475 per student.
Initial 0 answers insert per day before multiplying by 4; the text does
not say each day. Retain the first alternative-scope objection and
second conventional support, not a revised recovery estimate.

\textbf{C35 Checkpoints.} Interior route checkpoints at mile 1 and mile
25 enclose 24 miles and 3 intervening gaps, hence 8 miles. The closing
2-mile arc is outside those gaps. All 8 initials give 8; all 4 gold
matches 6 divide by 4 and are wrong. Screen 1 has an internally
contradictory parenthetical despite its correct final 8.

\textbf{C37 Walking distance.} Summed person-distance is 5 × 3 × 6+12+6
= 108; shared outings counted once give 36. The aggregate quantity is
genuinely different. Initial 0-6 are correct under person-mile reading;
initial 7 incorrectly repeats weekly extras and gets 198. Neither
gold-matching response proves the 108 answers wrong.

\textbf{C38 Hank reading.} 5 × (30+60)+2 × 2 × (30+60) = 810 minutes.
Reference final sentence mislabels subtotals but final 810 is correct.
Initial 630/690/1350 claims are wrong; fresh 2 visibly supplies the
correct 450+360 calculation.

\textbf{C47 Cake and ham.} Cake-day overlap with any ham day during the
week has probability 3/5 = 60\%. A specified/randomly sampled test day
has joint probability 3/25 = 12\%. Both reviewer rounds retain
sampling-unit ambiguity; all 5 fresh 12 matches use the latter event.

\textbf{C50 Reggie marbles.} With starting Reggie count R and losses
L,90 = R+10 × (9-2 L), so R = 20 L. His friend's 100 does not set R.
Multiple feasible starts/losses exist. Gold's net-loss shortcut is
invalid; all 3 fresh 1 matches have defective justifications. There is
no uniquely correct loss count.

asterisk marks a retained first/second category disagreement. E marks a
frozen parser event; N marks a conservative numeric witness. Neither

\section{Public evidence
availability}\label{public-evidence-availability}

The included case ledger preserves all sixty-four classifications, all
eleven parser-defined events, scientific hashes and review
disagreements. Source labels S1-S9 refer to frozen scientific summaries
and reviews; the public reference-binding file records their
descriptions and checksums without private operational paths. These
checksum references do not assert that every underlying payload is
publicly included. Operational closeout records, runtime/session
identifiers, raw archive transfer bindings and allocation details are
excluded. S12 and S13 identify the retained generation configuration and
scientific manifest; this public derivative reports their scientific
settings in the methodology without publishing their private transfer
wrappers.



\chapter{Source Reference Map}\label{source-reference-map}

This map collects source references retained in the sanitized
manuscript. A reference is not a promise that its payload is bundled or
publicly available. Repository-relative scientific paths are retained
where safe; private operational identifiers and source-export paths are
omitted. The public availability manifest distinguishes included
document sources from withheld evidence categories. Original records
have not been regenerated to fill gaps, and no complete public
provenance or universal experiment-coverage claim is made. Table sources
remain under their tables.

\chapter{Public Coverage and Source
Availability}\label{public-coverage-and-source-availability}

This appendix is the publication-safe derivative of a bounded source
audit completed on 3 October 2026. It reports scientific source
availability and limitations. It does not publish authenticated provider
inventories, private project names, account-wide activity totals,
collaborator identities or operational access correspondence. The
underlying private audit records are withheld; these paragraphs do not
turn their contents into independently reproducible public evidence.

\section{Counting units and publication
boundary}\label{counting-units-and-publication-boundary}

A scientific execution, a logging record, a training identity, a
checkpoint revision and a claim-to-source link are different units. One
execution can generate multiple records, retries and checkpoint
versions. These units must not be added as independent trials. Appendix
A retains scientific study scope and qualitative registry limitations;
no account-wide inventory is included.

Provider inventory work used bounded pagination, immutable revisions
where available, explicit source joins and missing-field handling. An
inventory match establishes recorded identity within the inspected
scope, not successful training, benchmark correctness or availability of
all historical weights. Unavailable or inaccessible records, unresolved
joins, unproven consumer-list exhaustion and missing history remain
limitations. Account-wide counts and exact operational identities are
omitted.

The editable sources and selected scientific case ledgers in this
package can rebuild the public document. They cannot reproduce every
experiment. The source-reference map is a map of reported scientific
sources, including references whose original payloads are retained
outside this public package. The public availability manifest is the
authority on which files are actually included.

\section{Bounded checkpoint and provider
evidence}\label{bounded-checkpoint-and-provider-evidence}

Training metadata, checkpoint bytes and an executable serving route are
distinct evidence. This public derivative does not publish private
provider state, checkpoint-retention findings or exact execution
identities. The public package cannot reproduce the original managed
execution route, and no current account or private repository
availability is asserted.

Public upstream model and benchmark revision references remain where
scientifically relevant. Published Git or LFS identifiers are metadata,
not a fresh byte-integrity test of a large tensor payload. Small public
card, configuration and manifest checks do not establish numerical
equivalence across serving stacks. No training, sampling or benchmark
evaluation was rerun by this source audit. Per-step metric histories
were not reanalysed.

\section{Public P11 confirmatory
bindings}\label{public-p11-confirmatory-bindings}

The forty public manifest bindings below belong to P11, the
five-algorithm by eight-seed confirmatory study. The repository naming
convention includes e1, but these are not the E1 SWE-bench lane of the
later fourteen-suite campaign. At the recorded audit date, all forty
public pinned commits matched the public heads, and all forty downloaded
run manifests matched both their expected SHA-256 and retained local
bytes. The companion public binding file retains scientific units,
immutable commit and checksum fields. Execution-specific repository
access paths are omitted to avoid publishing embedded provider
identifiers; this limits independent public replay from this package
alone.

Each accepted unit records checkpoints at steps 5, 10, 15, 20, 25 and 30
plus a final adapter. Optional evaluation-resume sidecars were recorded
for 36 of 40 units; that difference does not remove the matching final
manifests. These are forty accepted scientific units, not forty
additional trials inferred from provider logging.

The manifests specify thirty optimizer steps. P11 therefore does not
satisfy the report's Tier A threshold of at least one hundred steps,
despite its eight-seed design. The reported contrast, confidence
interval, power estimate and null result remain unchanged. Failed and
superseded attempts are not pooled with the accepted forty units.

\section{Recovered campaign evidence}\label{recovered-campaign-evidence}

Six unchanged original files resolved five named primary-file gaps in
the retained-source audit. Their private filesystem and export metadata
are not included here. The scientific availability result is narrower
than validation of every claim: LAB-Bench records 450 correct of 1,967;
AgentDojo records 88 utility passes across 97 benign episodes, with
security evaluation false and no held-out claim. The recovered
training-identity note explicitly disclaims content decontamination and
does not provide the dirty source diff.

Bounded stored-payload checks cover 29 explicit file-reference records
across 27 targets, 1,979 E8 archive/local bindings and 525 E10
archive-member bindings. The selected 1,967 E8 grades and 97 E10 traces
reproduce the stored success counts of 450 and 88. E8's 1,259
unparsed-answer total remains diagnostic-supported rather than
independently recalculated from the reduced grade rows. These are
structural, hash and tally checks, not model execution, scorer reruns or
independent semantic grading.

For E14, the source-side record contains 4,428 dispositions, 4,426
accepted judged rows and 2,271 successes. The official judged-row score
is 2,271/4,426 = 51.31\%; the all-disposition diagnostic is 2,271/4,428
= 51.29\%, counting two missing-verdict rows as failures. The recovered
terminal note incorrectly associates the official decimal with the full
denominator. The erratum remains explicit, the original source is
unchanged, and strict-contract score is not inferred from terminal
closure.

The recovered finish-era chronology precedes the later E4 rerun and
cannot verify that later execution. The later one-hundred-trial
zero-reward account remains a separate reported result with
raw-trajectory limits. Original training-log payloads, the dirty source
diff and content-level decontamination remain unresolved.

\section{Preserved C1 result and residual
limits}\label{preserved-c1-result-and-residual-limits}

The C1 parser-defined endpoint remains 11/64. The conservative review
supports three numerical recovery witnesses on the same denominator;
these counts are separate and neither establishes training improvement.
The sixty-four reference classifications, eleven event records,
scientific hashes and disagreements remain in the included case ledger.
Historical V2 findings remain separate and unpooled.

This package excludes private provider exports, authenticated inventory
and reconciliation ledgers, raw access/budget chronologies, private
notebook links, original recovery-export wrappers, personal signature
data and account identifiers. Omission is a publication boundary, not
evidence that a scientific result failed. Conversely, a retained source
reference or checksum is not proof that the cited bytes are publicly
available or that all claims have been independently verified. The
public PDF has a distinct title and filename and does not replace the
preserved historical manuscript or certify institutional approval.
